def resolve_agent_mode(step: str, args: argparse.Namespace, *, home: Path | None = None) -> tuple[str, str, bool, str | None]:
    """Returns (agent, mode, refreshed, model).

    Both agents default to persistent sessions.  Use --fresh to start a new
    persistent session (break continuity) or --ephemeral for a truly one-off
    call with no session saved.

    The model is extracted from compound agent specs (e.g. 'hermes:openai/gpt-5')
    or from --phase-model / --hermes CLI flags. None means use agent default.
    """
    model = None

    # Check --phase-model overrides first (highest priority)
    phase_models = getattr(args, "phase_model", None) or []
    for pm in phase_models:
        if "=" in pm:
            pm_step, pm_spec = pm.split("=", 1)
            if pm_step == step:
                agent, model = parse_agent_spec(pm_spec)
                break
    else:
        # Check --hermes flag
        hermes_flag = getattr(args, "hermes", None)
        if hermes_flag is not None:
            agent = "hermes"
            if isinstance(hermes_flag, str) and hermes_flag:
                model = hermes_flag
        else:
            # Check explicit --agent flag
            explicit = args.agent
            if explicit:
                agent, model = parse_agent_spec(explicit)
            else:
                # Fall back to config / defaults
                config = load_config(home)
                spec = config.get("agents", {}).get(step) or DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING[step]
                agent, model = parse_agent_spec(spec)

    # Validate agent availability
    explicit_agent = args.agent  # was an explicit --agent flag used?
    if not _is_agent_available(agent):
        # If explicitly requested (via --agent), fail immediately
        if explicit_agent and not any(pm.startswith(f"{step}=") for pm in (getattr(args, "phase_model", None) or [])):
            if agent == "hermes":
                raise CliError(
                    "agent_deps_missing",
                    "hermes backend requires: pip install 'megaplan-harness[agent]'",
                )
            raise CliError("agent_not_found", f"Agent '{agent}' not found on PATH")
        # For hermes via --hermes flag, give a specific error
        if getattr(args, "hermes", None) is not None or agent == "hermes":
            raise CliError(
                "agent_deps_missing",
                "hermes backend requires: pip install 'megaplan-harness[agent]'",
            )
        # Try fallback
        available = detect_available_agents()
        if not available:
            raise CliError(
                "agent_not_found",
                "No supported agents found. Install claude or codex, or pip install 'megaplan-harness[agent]' for hermes.",
```

def profile_to_phase_models(profile: dict[str, str]) -> list[str]:
    return [f"{phase}={spec}" for phase, spec in profile.items()]


def apply_profile_expansion(
    args: argparse.Namespace,
    project_dir: Path | None,
    state: dict | None = None,
) -> argparse.Namespace:

    if profile_name:
        profiles = load_profiles(project_dir=project_dir)
        resolved = resolve_profile(profile_name, profiles)
        phase_models.extend(profile_to_phase_models(resolved))

## Profile Flow in Megaplan: Complete Analysis

Based on my search through the codebase, here's exactly how profiles work:

### 1. **How `--profile` is Handled in Init** (`megaplan/handlers/init.py` & `megaplan/cli.py`)

**In `cli.py` (parser definition, lines 932-933):**
- The `--profile` flag is registered for the `init` command
- It accepts a string argument: profile name

**In `handlers/init.py` (init execution, line 33):**
```python
apply_profile_expansion(args, project_dir)
```
- Immediately called at the start of `handle_init()`
- Expands the profile name into individual `--phase-model` overrides
- These overrides are merged into `args.phase_model` as a list

**In `handlers/init.py` (state persistence, lines 152-153):**
```python
if getattr(args, "profile", None):
    state["config"]["profile"] = args.profile
```
- If a profile name was provided, it's **stored directly in state.json** under `state["config"]["profile"]`
- This is critical: the profile name itself is persisted, not the expanded phase models

**Also persisted (lines 163-165):**
```python
phase_models = list(getattr(args, "phase_model", None) or [])
if phase_models:
    state["config"]["phase_model"] = phase_models
```
- The expanded `--phase-model` list is also saved in `state["config"]["phase_model"]` for persistence

---

### 2. **Where Profile Config Gets Stored in state.json**

**Location:** `state["config"]["profile"]` (string: profile name)

**Example from a real plan** (`add-a-short-docstring-comment-20260424-0104/state.json`, lines 7-14):
```json
"config": {
  "project_dir": "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan",
  "auto_approve": true,
  "robustness": "standard",
  "mode": "code",
  "agent": "",
  "profile": "all-kimi"
}
```

**Comparison: state.json without profile** (from the first example):
```json
"config": {
  "max_iterations": 3,
  "budget_usd": 25.0,
  "project_dir": "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/devtools"
}
```
- When no profile is set, the `"profile"` key is simply absent from the config dict
- The only difference is the presence/absence of `"profile": "<name>"` 

**Optional persistence:** `state["config"]["phase_model"]` (list of strings)
- When `--phase-model` overrides are passed (from profile expansion or direct CLI), they're stored as a list like `["plan=claude", "critique=codex"]`

---

### 3. **How Profile Config is Used Later for Agent Selection**

**The flow for each phase (plan, critique, gate, finalize, execute, review, etc.):**

**Step 1: In handlers (e.g., `megaplan/handlers/shared.py`, line 157):**
```python
apply_profile_expansion(args, Path(state["config"]["project_dir"]), state=state)
```
- Called in `_run_worker()` before agent resolution
- **Critically:** `apply_profile_expansion()` reads the profile name from **state**, not args (see below)

**Step 2: In `megaplan/profiles/__init__.py` (`apply_profile_expansion()`, lines 143-174):**
```python
def apply_profile_expansion(
    args: argparse.Namespace,
    project_dir: Path | None,
    state: dict | None = None,
) -> argparse.Namespace:
    if getattr(args, "_profile_applied", False):
        return args

    profile_name = getattr(args, "profile", None)
    if profile_name is None and state is not None:
        # Fallback: read profile from state if not in args
        profile_name = (state.get("config") or {}).get("profile")
    
    phase_models = list(getattr(args, "phase_model", None) or [])

    if profile_name:
        profiles = load_profiles(project_dir=project_dir)
        resolved = resolve_profile(profile_name, profiles)
        phase_models.extend(profile_to_phase_models(resolved))
```

**Key insight:** 
- The `state` fallback is necessary because the auto-driver spawns each phase as a **fresh subprocess** that doesn't receive the `--profile` CLI flag
- Each phase handler recovers the profile name from `state["config"]["profile"]`
- The profile is then expanded into `--phase-model` overrides like `plan=claude`, `critique=codex`

**Step 3: In `megaplan/workers.py` (`resolve_agent_mode()`, lines ~370-420):**
```python
def resolve_agent_mode(step: str, args: argparse.Namespace, *, home: Path | None = None) -> tuple[str, str, bool, str | None]:
    """Returns (agent, mode, refreshed, model)."""
    model = None

    # Priority 1: Check --phase-model overrides (from profile expansion)
    phase_models = getattr(args, "phase_model", None) or []
    for pm in phase_models:
        if "=" in pm:
            pm_step, pm_spec = pm.split("=", 1)
            if pm_step == step:
                agent, model = parse_agent_spec(pm_spec)
                break
    else:
        # Priority 2: Check --hermes flag
        hermes_flag = getattr(args, "hermes", None)
        if hermes_flag is not None:
            agent = "hermes"
            if isinstance(hermes_flag, str) and hermes_flag:
                model = hermes_flag
        else:
            # Priority 3: Check explicit --agent flag
            explicit = args.agent
            if explicit:
                agent, model = parse_agent_spec(explicit)
            else:
                # Priority 4: Fall back to config or defaults
                config = load_config(home)
                spec = config.get("agents", {}).get(step) or DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING[step]
                agent, model = parse_agent_spec(spec)
```

**Agent selection priority (highest to lowest):**
1. `--phase-model` overrides (comes from profile expansion)
2. `--hermes` flag 
3. `--agent` flag
4. Global config (`~/.megaplan/config.json` → `config["agents"][step]`)
5. Built-in defaults (`DEFAULT_AGENT_ROUTING`)

---

### 4. **Profile Config Structure Comparison**

**With profile set (e.g., `all-kimi`):**
```json
{
  "config": {
    "project_dir": "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan",
    "auto_approve": true,
    "robustness": "standard",
    "mode": "code",
    "strict_notes": false,
    "agent": "",
    "profile": "all-kimi",
    "phase_model": [
      "plan=hermes:kimi",
      "prep=hermes:kimi",
      "critique=hermes:kimi",
      "revise=hermes:kimi",
      "gate=hermes:kimi",
      "finalize=hermes:kimi",
      "execute=hermes:kimi",
      "loop_plan=hermes:kimi",
      "loop_execute=hermes:kimi",
      "review=hermes:kimi",
      "tiebreaker_researcher=hermes:kimi",
      "tiebreaker_challenger=hermes:kimi"
    ]
  }
}
```

**Without profile (default):**
```json
{
  "config": {
    "project_dir": "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/devtools",
    "auto_approve": false,
    "robustness": "standard",
    "mode": "code",
    "strict_notes": false,
    "agent": ""
  }
}
```

**Key difference:** 
- Presence of `"profile"` string (the profile name)
- Presence of `"phase_model"` list (expanded agent specs per phase)

### 5. **Enabling Profile Changes After Init**

To add support for changing profiles after init, you would need to:

1. **New CLI command:** Add a `megaplan override set-profile <name>` or similar
2. **Implementation:** 
   - Read and validate the profile name
   - Update `state["config"]["profile"]` to the new profile name
   - Optionally update or clear `state["config"]["phase_model"]` (handlers will re-expand on next phase)
   - Save the state file
3. **Phase handlers:** Already support this via `apply_profile_expansion()` fallback — they will automatically use the new profile from state on the next phase invocation

**File paths to examine for implementation:**
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/cli.py` - add CLI parser for override
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/handlers/override.py` - add handler logic
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/_core/` - state save/load utilities

3	  "idea": "# Sprint 1a \u2014 agent_kit core + invocation mode\n\nBuild the foundational Python package for Arnold, a Discord planning bot. This sprint proves the substrate against the cheapest possible adapter (CLI over SQLite) before Discord/Supabase land in Sprint 1b.\n\n**Full spec is at `planning-bot-spec.md` in this repo root. Refer to it for complete data model schemas, tool signatures, and architectural details.**\n\n## Supabase (for later sprints)\n- URL: https://yhwflvadmefhkshwbfnf.supabase.co\n- Service key: [REDACTED_SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_JWT]\n\n## Scope\n\n- `agent_kit/` package skeleton:\n  - `ports.py` \u2014 Transport, Store, Model, Blob protocol definitions (abstract interfaces)\n  - `loop.py` \u2014 transport-agnostic `run_turn` function\n  - `tool_kit.py` \u2014 tool registry + audit-wrap decorator\n  - `ledger.py` \u2014 event store skeleton\n- `Envelope` dataclass + JSON schema (outcome: completed/blocked_on_caller/errored/aborted, events array, state_delta, reply)\n- CLI transport adapter (pull mode): `arnold turn --epic <id> --input \"text\"` command; NDJSON event streaming with `--stream-events`; exit codes per envelope outcome (0=completed, 2=blocked, 1=errored, 3=aborted)\n- SQLite store adapter: schema for epics, messages, bot_turns, tool_calls, system_logs tables (resident-mode-only columns nullable); migrations under `agent_kit/store/migrations/sqlite/`\n- Anthropic model adapter: Claude Opus 4.7 wired to the loop\n- Centralized logger module writing to system_logs via the Store port\n- Agentic loop skeleton: receive \u2192 reason \u2192 respond, returns an envelope\n- Minimal tool surface: `send_message` (writes to reply buffer in invocation mode), `set_activity` (emits event)\n- Test harness: pytest setup, ephemeral SQLite per test, mocked Anthropic client\n\n## Key Data Model Tables (this sprint)\n\n### epics\nid, title, goal, body (markdown), state (shaping|sprinting|planned|paused|archived), created_at, last_edited_at, last_active_at, planned_at\n\n### messages\nid, epic_id, direction (inbound|outbound), content, sent_at, discord_message_id (unique), has_code_attachment, has_image_attachment, in_burst_with, was_voice_message, audio_storage_url, transcription_metadata\n\n### bot_turns\nid, epic_id, triggered_by_message_ids (uuid array), prompt_snapshot, prompt_version, reasoning, final_output_message_id, status_message_id, status (in_progress|completed|failed|abandoned), state_at_turn, started_at, completed_at, model_version\n\n### tool_calls\nid, turn_id, tool_name, operation_kind (read|write), arguments (json), result (json), called_at, duration_ms\n\n### system_logs\nid, level (debug|info|warn|error), category, event_type, message, details (json), turn_id, epic_id, occurred_at\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n\n- `arnold turn --epic <id> --input \"hello\"` returns a valid envelope on stdout; exit code 0 for completed, 2 for blocked_on_caller, 1 for errored, 3 for aborted\n- Every Anthropic call recorded in bot_turns; every tool call recorded in tool_calls; envelope events array matches tool_calls rows\n- Same input + same store state + same model seed \u2192 identical state_delta (deterministic-structure test)\n- Python `run_turn(...)` produces a byte-equivalent envelope to the CLI for the same input\n- `--stream-events` emits NDJSON to stderr with one event per tool call; final envelope on stdout matches\n- Test suite runs green via pytest against ephemeral SQLite\n\n## Tests\n\n- Unit: envelope schema validation; Store port contract tests against SQLite impl; tool_kit audit-wrap atomicity\n- Integration: full receive\u2192reason\u2192respond loop with mocked Anthropic; verify envelope contents and DB state\n- Integration: CLI \u2194 Python equivalence \u2014 invoke same input both ways, assert envelopes match (modulo non-deterministic reply text)\n\n## Tech Stack\n- Python 3.11+\n- pytest for testing\n- anthropic SDK for Claude Opus 4.7\n- SQLite for local store\n- Click or argparse for CLI\n- dataclasses for data structures",

enforces heading hierarchy (# for title only, ## for sections, ### for sub-headings)\n- Turn-end epic outline emitted to system_logs at info level\n- Default body template (design doc) \u2014 Goal, Principles, Context, Key Decisions, Open Questions, Deliverable\n- `edit_epic` tool \u2014 body (whole + section ops) + checklist; supports:\n  - Write whole body\n  - Write specific sections\n  - Append to a section\n  - Add new section with position\n  - Rename/remove sections\n  - `expected_diff` parameter for server-enforced diff verification (unified diff format)\n- `create_epic`, `revert` (transaction-grouped), `render_epic` tools\n- Read tools: `get_epic` (with section addressing), `get_section_names`, `get_history`, `get_self_understanding`\n- History tools: `get_epic_at_time`, `get_recent_turns`, `search_tool_calls`\n- Default checklist seed \u2014 18 items with adaptation logic\n- Title and goal are derived columns from body parsing (# Title and ## Goal first paragraph)\n\n## Key Data Model\n\n### checklist_items\nid, epic_id, content, status (open|done|skipped|superseded), position, source (bot_inferred|user_requested|carried_over|default_seed|second_opinion), skip_reason, superseded_by_item_id, created_at, completed_at\n\n### epic_events\nid, epic_id, transaction_id (uuid), event_type, summary, prior_state (json), turn_id, occurred_at\nEvent types: body_edit, checklist_change, sprints_change, state_change, forced_handoff, created, code_referenced, codebase_added, image_generated, second_opinion_requested, reverted_to, sprint_status_change\n\n## Body Parser Rules\n- Section boundaries are ## headings (level-2 markdown)\n- Section names are case-sensitive\n- Pre-section content is \"_preamble\"\n- Sub-sections (### and below) are part of parent section\n- No ## headings \u2192 whole body is _preamble\n- # Title and ## Goal are required structural elements; missing \u2192 write rejected\n- Code blocks containing ## are NOT section boundaries\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n\n- Create an epic via natural language \u2192 epics row created, default checklist seeded with 18 items, body initialized\n- 10-turn scripted conversation (mocked Anthropic) produces body with all 6 default sections\n- Section-level edit \u2192 only that section changes; other sections byte-identical\n- Whole body edit \u2192 diff captured in event; revert restores prior version exactly\n- \"revert that\" \u2192 most recent transaction undone, new reverted_to event logged\n- expected_diff mismatch \u2192 server refuses write, returns actual diff\n- expected_diff match \u2192 server commits normally\n- get_epic_at_time(epic_id, T) \u2192 returns body/checklist state as of time T\n- get_recent_turns(5) \u2192 returns 5 most recent turns with summaries\n- search_tool_calls(tool_name='edit_epic', epic_id=X) \u2192 returns matching calls\n- Turn-end system_logs row with event_type='epic_outline' containing title + section list + line counts\n\n## Tests\n- Unit: body parser (markdown \u2192 sections \u2192 markdown roundtrip is identity); section operations; edit_epic validation; transaction_id grouping; expected_diff comparison; epic-at-time replay\n- Integration: 10-turn fixture conversation against local Supabase with mocked Anthropic; Refer to Epic Selection, References to Bot's Recent Output, User Modes, Showing the Bot's Understanding sections.**\n\n## Supabase\n- URL: https://yhwflvadmefhkshwbfnf.supabase.co\n- Service key: [REDACTED_SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_JWT]\n\n## Scope\n\n- Epic selection heuristic \u2014 24h most-recent default; bot picks most recently edited epic within 24h on ambiguous messages\n- Epic switching with announcements (outbound message contains epic title when switching)\n- `list_epics`, `search_epics`, `search_messages` tools\n- Full-text search index on messages.content\n- Ambiguity handling \u2014 bot asks when epic context is unclear\n- Reference resolution \u2014 \"the second one\" / \"that point\" parsed against last outbound bot message\n- Conversation gap acknowledgment\n- User mode reading \u2014 concrete behaviors per mode:\n  - Deep-thinking: persona dialed down, measured and substantive\n  - Brainstorming: more energy, exploratory\n  - Executing: direct, minimal elaboration\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n\n- 5 epics active \u2192 bot picks most recently edited within 24h on ambiguous messages (LLM-graded eval: \u226527/30 correct on canned scenarios)\n- \"the second one\" / \"that point\" reference resolution against last bot message (unit tests: \u22659/10 on varied last-message structures)\n- Switching epics triggers announcement with epic title in outbound message\n- \"show me what you know about X\" \u2192 structured summary with all relevant sections\n- Full-text search across messages returns relevant matches (10 canned queries with expected hit IDs)\n\n## Tests\n- Unit: epic selection heuristic across epic-set fixtures; reference resolver against varied bot output; mode signal detection\n- Integration: multi-epic switching, search retrieval against seeded message corpus\n- LLM-graded eval: ambiguity handling \u2014 bot asks (vs guesses) on 10 deliberately ambiguous cases",

363	  "signals": {
364	    "iteration": 5,
365	    "idea": "# Sprint 1a \u2014 agent_kit core + invocation mode\n\nBuild the foundational Python package for Arnold, a Discord planning bot. This sprint proves the substrate against the cheapest possible adapter (CLI over SQLite) before Discord/Supabase land in Sprint 1b.\n\n**Full spec is at `planning-bot-spec.md` in this repo root. Refer to it for complete data model schemas, tool signatures, and architectural details.**\n\n## Supabase (for later sprints)\n- URL: https://yhwflvadmefhkshwbfnf.supabase.co\n- Service key: [REDACTED_SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_JWT]\n\n## Scope\n\n- `agent_kit/` package skeleton:\n  - `ports.py` \u2014 Transport, Store, Model, Blob protocol definitions (abstract interfaces)\n  - `loop.py` \u2014 transport-agnostic `run_turn` function\n  - `tool_kit.py` \u2014 tool registry + audit-wrap decorator\n  - `ledger.py` \u2014 event store skeleton\n- `Envelope` dataclass + JSON schema (outcome: completed/blocked_on_caller/errored/aborted, events array, state_delta, reply)\n- CLI transport adapter (pull mode): `arnold turn --epic <id> --input \"text\"` command; NDJSON event streaming with `--stream-events`; exit codes per envelope outcome (0=completed, 2=blocked, 1=errored, 3=aborted)\n- SQLite store adapter: schema for epics, messages, bot_turns, tool_calls, system_logs tables (resident-mode-only columns nullable); migrations under `agent_kit/store/migrations/sqlite/`\n- Anthropic model adapter: Claude Opus 4.7 wired to the loop\n- Centralized logger module writing to system_logs via the Store port\n- Agentic loop skeleton: receive \u2192 reason \u2192 respond, returns an envelope\n- Minimal tool surface: `send_message` (writes to reply buffer in invocation mode), `set_activity` (emits event)\n- Test harness: pytest setup, ephemeral SQLite per test, mocked Anthropic client\n\n## Key Data Model Tables (this sprint)\n\n### epics\nid, title, goal, body (markdown), state (shaping|sprinting|planned|paused|archived), created_at, last_edited_at, last_active_at, planned_at\n\n### messages\nid, epic_id, direction (inbound|outbound), content, sent_at, discord_message_id (unique), has_code_attachment, has_image_attachment, in_burst_with, was_voice_message, audio_storage_url, transcription_metadata\n\n### bot_turns\nid, epic_id, triggered_by_message_ids (uuid array), prompt_snapshot, prompt_version, reasoning, final_output_message_id, status_message_id, status (in_progress|completed|failed|abandoned), state_at_turn, started_at, completed_at, model_version\n\n### tool_calls\nid, turn_id, tool_name, operation_kind (read|write), arguments (json), result (json), called_at, duration_ms\n\n### system_logs\nid, level (debug|info|warn|error), category, event_type, message, details (json), turn_id, epic_id, occurred_at\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n\n- `arnold turn --epic <id> --input \"hello\"` returns a valid envelope on stdout; exit code 0 for completed, 2 for blocked_on_caller, 1 for errored, 3 for aborted\n- Every Anthropic call recorded in bot_turns; every tool call recorded in tool_calls; envelope events array matches tool_calls rows\n- Same input + same store state + same model seed \u2192 identical state_delta (deterministic-structure test)\n- Python `run_turn(...)` produces a byte-equivalent envelope to the CLI for the same input\n- `--stream-events` emits NDJSON to stderr with one event per tool call; final envelope on stdout matches\n- Test suite runs green via pytest against ephemeral SQLite\n\n## Tests\n\n- Unit: envelope schema validation; Store port contract tests against SQLite impl; tool_kit audit-wrap atomicity\n- Integration: full receive\u2192reason\u2192respond loop with mocked Anthropic; verify envelope contents and DB state\n- Integration: CLI \u2194 Python equivalence \u2014 invoke same input both ways, assert envelopes match (modulo non-deterministic reply text)\n\n## Tech Stack\n- Python 3.11+\n- pytest for testing\n- anthropic SDK for Claude Opus 4.7\n- SQLite for local store\n- Click or argparse for CLI\n- dataclasses for data structures",

408	        "resolution": "Revised the plan to address all 14 open flags: (1) added a dedicated step locking the `megaplan.arnold` public import path with `run_turn`, `arun_turn`, and `Envelope` re-exports plus an import-path test (FLAG-002, correctness-1); (2) added an explicit `agent_kit/ledger.py` skeleton step and an `external_requests` table in migrations, with a written decision that scaffolding ships in 1a and reconciliation is deferred to 1b (FLAG-003, issue_hints-2, scope-1); (3) added a local Blob store, an attachment ingestion module, repeatable CLI `--attach`, Python `attachments=`, and attachment integration tests, with audio gracefully erroring until 1b (FLAG-001, issue_hints-1, callers-1); (4) tightened the determinism story: `state_delta` byte-equivalence is asserted unconditionally, while CLI\u2194Python equivalence is asserted on a `serialize_for_diff` projection that strips `reply`/`events.text`/timestamps (correctness-2); (5) removed the `bot_turns.current_activity` mutation from invocation-mode `set_activity` to honor mode-divergent semantics (correctness-3); (6) promoted model-string configurability from an assumption to an explicit step \u2014 adapter takes it as a constructor argument and the CLI reads `ARNOLD_MODEL_ID`, with no hardcoding in tests (all_locations-1); (7) restructured `tests/store_contract.py` as a parametrized, store-agnostic suite reusable unchanged in 1b (all_locations-2); (8) defined a real abort path via cooperative cancellation flag plus CLI `SIGINT` handler, with an integration test that exercises the `aborted` exit code (callers-2). The `defer_to_caller` tool is retained (scope-2 confirmed). Plan structure, scope, and execution order preserved; no scope creep beyond what the locked spec requires."

413	        "resolution": "Narrowed the plan back to the user's sprint idea and addressed the recurring debt flags. (2) `run_turn` now writes one `external_requests` row per main Anthropic call (`tool_call_id=null`, provider='anthropic', pending\u2192confirmed) so the ledger captures the only guaranteed external API call in Sprint 1a; reconciliation logic stays in 1b (FLAG-003, scope-1, scope). (3) Tightened streaming equivalence: streamed NDJSON is byte-identical to the envelope's `events` array; `serialize_for_diff` is now used only for CLI\u2194Python envelope equivalence, never for stream-vs-envelope comparison (correctness-1). 427	        "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: Model-call idempotency: Step 12 records an `external_requests` row before each main `Model.complete_turn(...)` call using the system idempotency key formula `sha256(turn_id + ':system:' + provider + ':' + endpoint)[:16]`. If one turn performs multiple model calls during tool-use chaining, those rows will collide on `UNIQUE(idempotency_key)` unless the implementation guarantees exactly one `Model.complete_turn` call per turn or includes an attempt/sequence discriminator in the keying strategy.",
428	        "resolution": "Two correctness fixes; the disputed attachment flags stay settled per SD-010. (1) **Idempotency-key collision (correctness-1, FLAG-008):** Step 7's system idempotency-key formula now includes a per-turn `system_seq` ordinal, so multi-call tool-use turns produce distinct keys instead of colliding on `UNIQUE(idempotency_key)`. The loop (Step 12) owns the counter, increments it per `Model.complete_turn(...)` call, and passes it via `Ledger.record_pending(..., system_seq=...)`. `FakeModel` is required to support multi-call sequences (Step 11) and a new integration test (Step 15.5 'tool-use chaining') asserts two distinct rows with `system_seq=1` and `system_seq=2` both transition `pending\u2192confirmed`. The extension of the spec's bare formula is documented in the ledger module and the plan notes. (2) **`set_activity` event shape (correctness-2):** the tool registry now carries an `event_kind` per entry (Step 9). `set_activity` is registered with `event_kind='activity'`; `send_message` and `defer_to_caller` with `'tool_call'`. The audit wrapper writes a `tool_calls` row (preserving the audit invariant) and emits exactly one envelope event whose `kind` matches the registry entry. Step 10 explicitly states `set_activity` does not emit an additional `tool_call`-kind event, and Step 15.6 asserts the event-shape invariant. The acceptance criterion is restated in Step 2.4 and Step 10.4: every `tool_calls` row has exactly one envelope event with matching `tool_call_id`, regardless of `kind`. Plan structure preserved."

120	- [x] **T12:** Implement `run_turn` in `agent_kit/loop.py`. Sequence: (1) Acquire per-epic lock via Store; if contended return `errored` envelope with `error.code='epic_locked'`. (5) Execute requested tools through audit wrapper until model returns final text, calls `defer_to_caller`, or errors. Tool-use chaining loops to step 4. (6) If model returns final text without `send_message`, synthesize one so `reply` is never empty for `completed`. (7) Compute `state_delta` as a stable empty artifact delta with byte-deterministic structure. (8) Mark turn `completed`/`failed`/`abandoned`, release lock, return byte-stable `Envelope`. (9) `on_event` callback called once per appended event, in envelope order. (10) **Abort path:** install cooperative cancellation flag (set by CLI on SIGINT or by Python caller via optional `cancel_event: threading.Event`). Between tool steps, check the flag; if set, mark `bot_turns.status='abandoned'`, release lock, return envelope with `outcome='aborted'`. Both row-status `abandoned` and envelope-outcome `aborted` coexist by design.

177	## Watch Items
178	
179	- DO NOT commit the Supabase service key from the idea file anywhere — code, tests, fixtures, logs, prompts, or commit messages. The idea file itself contains it; treat that file as read-only and do not echo the key downstream.
180	- Idempotency-key extension is intentional: the system formula adds a per-turn `system_seq` ordinal beyond the spec's bare formula. Document it explicitly at the top of `agent_kit/ledger.py`. Sprint 1b reconciliation must use the SAME formula — do not silently revert it.
181	- Audit invariant: every `tool_calls` row has EXACTLY ONE envelope event with matching `tool_call_id`, regardless of `kind`. `set_activity` writes a `tool_calls` row AND emits one `activity` event — not two events, not zero, not a `tool_call`-kind event.
182	- `set_activity` in invocation mode does NOT mutate `bot_turns.current_activity` (mode-divergent semantics per spec, SD-005). Only emits the activity event + audit row.
183	- Streaming NDJSON must be byte-identical to the envelope's final `events` array — same encoder, same objects, same order, no projection. `serialize_for_diff` is ONLY for CLI↔Python envelope equivalence, never for stream comparison.
184	- Abort path: `bot_turns.status='abandoned'` AND envelope `outcome='aborted'` coexist by design. Both must be asserted; a valid envelope still prints to stdout with exit code 3.
185	- Ledger lifecycle: ProviderError → `mark_failed` (status=failed, error_details set). Transport/SDK exception → row stays `pending` (1b reconciliation). Do not conflate these — tests assert both paths separately.
186	- FakeModel MUST support multi-call tool-use scripting so the `system_seq` discriminator is actually exercised — not just one-shot scripts.
187	- `AnthropicModel.model_id` is a constructor argument; `ARNOLD_MODEL_ID` is read in `arnold/cli.py` ONLY. No test or adapter hardcodes the id.
188	- `tests/store_contract.py` must be parametrized over a `store_factory` fixture and remain reusable unchanged with a Supabase factory in 1b.
189	- `run_turn` is the single source of truth; CLI is a thin wrapper. No duplicated turn orchestration in `arnold/cli.py`.
190	- Sprint 1b seams: store / model / transport / blob behind ports. No Discord or Supabase assumptions leak into `run_turn`.
191	- Attachments deferred per SD-010: NO `--attach` CLI flag, NO Python `attachments=` arg, NO `LocalBlobStore`, NO attachment ingestion or tests in 1a. Only the `Blob` port ships. Reviewers may re-flag this — settled, do not relitigate.
192	- Tests run fully offline against ephemeral SQLite + FakeModel; no network access required. Do not introduce real Anthropic calls in CI tests.
193	- `megaplan` is a regular owned package with its own `__init__.py` (SD-011). The repo runs inside the megaplan harness which is a separate distribution; do not convert this to a namespace package.
194	- `external_requests` schema ships with the FULL spec column set in 1a (SD-013) including `endpoint`, `turn_id`, `provider_request_id`, `provider_response_summary`, `attempt_count`, `first_attempted_at`, `last_attempted_at`, `completed_at`, `error_details`, plus the spec's full index set including UNIQUE(idempotency_key).

262	## Meta
263	
264	This sprint creates a Python project from scratch in a documentation-only repo (`planning-bot-spec.md` + `ideas/`). No prior code exists, so there is no baseline test command to inherit. The plan has been through 5 critique iterations and the gate explicitly warned of high iteration count — execute as written, do not relitigate scope. Two settled-decision pivots are load-bearing and reviewers may re-flag them; treat as accepted: (1) attachments are deferred to 1b per SD-010 (no `--attach`, no `attachments=`, no `LocalBlobStore`); (2) the system idempotency-key formula is intentionally extended with `system_seq` beyond the spec's bare formula per SD-015. Document the latter divergence at the top of `agent_kit/ledger.py`. Two non-obvious correctness gotchas are easy to get wrong: (a) `set_activity` writes a `tool_calls` row AND emits exactly one event with `kind='activity'` — neither zero events nor two events nor a `tool_call`-kind event; (b) streaming NDJSON must be byte-identical to the envelope's `events` array using the same encoder + same objects — `serialize_for_diff` is ONLY for CLI↔Python comparison. The Anthropic adapter must distinguish `ProviderError` (→ `mark_failed`) from raw transport exceptions (→ row stays `pending` for 1b reconciliation); FakeModel must script both error modes plus multi-call sequences. Build order roughly mirrors the plan's execution order: skeleton → ports/envelope → store/migrations → ledger/logging → tool_kit/tools → model adapters → run_turn → CLI → tests. Tests run offline against FakeModel + ephemeral SQLite — never introduce real network calls. The Supabase service key in the idea file must NOT be committed downstream anywhere. The repo runs inside a separate `megaplan` harness distribution; the owned `megaplan` package is a regular package per SD-011 and they do not collide.

9	      "evidence": "The plan places the epic-selection module \"before or during response planning\" but does not call out the current resident routing/locking contract. `DiscordTransport.on_message` persists inbound rows with `_message_epic_id(message)`, which defaults to `discord_user_<author>` (`agent_kit/transport/discord.py`), `ArnoldResident.handle_transport_message` then coalesces by `payload[\"epic_id\"]` and calls `run_turn(epic_id=epic_id)` (`agent_kit/resident.py`), and `run_turn` acquires exactly that initial epic lock before loading hot context (`agent_kit/loop.py`). If Sprint 3 chooses a different real epic inside the model/planning phase, edits can proceed without holding the selected epic's lock, and inbound/outbound message rows may remain attached to the synthetic conversation key rather than the selected epic. The plan needs an explicit insertion point for selection before lock/turn creation, or a safe switch path that updates message/turn epic IDs and obtains/releases the selected epic lock."

The spec marks `search_messages` as a both-modes read tool, and current tool code depends on `Store` protocol methods (`agent_kit/ports.py`) with contract tests for both adapters. Here is the full audit of all 5 completed Arnold megaplan sprints:

---

## Sprint 1a: agent_kit core + invocation mode

**Goal:** Build foundational Python package for Arnold (ports, loop, envelope, SQLite store, CLI, tests).

**Plan quality:** Excellent. 5 critique iterations, weighted score decreased from 22.5 to 8.5. The critique caught real issues: import path unresolved (`megaplan.arnold`), idempotency-key collision under tool-use chaining, `set_activity` event-shape ambiguity, and external_requests schema completeness. Revisions addressed all of them. 16 settled decisions were documented.

**Critique quality:** Strong. The initial critique found 6 real flags (callable API import path, ledger skeleton missing, attachment scope tension, determinism underspec, etc.). These drove meaningful plan improvements. Some flags were appropriately recurring (attachment scope) and the gate eventually settled them.

**Gate quality:** Added value. Converged over 5 iterations. Score trajectory 22.5 -> 16.5 -> 11.0 -> 14.5 -> 8.5. Properly recognized the attachment scope question needed a tiebreaker and settled it. The gate appropriately warned about high iteration count and pushed for convergence.

**Execute quality:**
- Tasks: 16 planned, 16 done, 0 skipped, 0 blocked
- Source files: 29 files claimed but **0 in git diff** (execution audit skipped because "Project directory is not a git repository")
- This is the sandbox bug -- the executor ran in a Codex sandbox that was not a git repo, so the git diff was empty. However, the code was actually produced (the files exist in the repo).
- Tests: 22 tests passing

**Review quality:** Real review, not auto-approved. Verdict: approved. 23 criteria checked, all pass (3 deferred to human). Detailed task-by-task verdicts, 16 sense checks confirmed. Pre-check flags noted missing git diff, but the review still verified code by reading files directly.

**Idea coverage:** All acceptance criteria met per reviewer. CLI envelope, exit codes, tool auditing, state_delta determinism, CLI/Python equivalence, streaming NDJSON, test suite green. Attachment passing intentionally deferred per settled decision.

**Red flags:** Execution audit could not verify git diff (sandbox not a git repo). No actual diff-based verification was possible.

---

## Sprint 1b: Discord resident mode + robustness

**Goal:** Add Discord transport, Supabase store, message coalescing, voice/image support, live status, mid-turn handling, reconciliation.

**Plan quality:** Good. 4 iterations. Critique caught real issues: store contract mutation (plan changed the v1a contract suite instead of preserving it), mid-turn message handling divergence from spec, missing psycopg dependency, tool transaction/ledger ordering conflict.

**Critique quality:** Strong. Found meaningful correctness issues about external-request reconciliation implementability, tool-call atomicity vs external IO ordering, status lifecycle hook timing, and ToolContext dependency injection gaps.

**Gate quality:** Added value. Passed with appropriate warnings and 17 settled decisions documented.

**Execute quality:**
- Tasks: 23 planned, 22 done, 1 skipped (user_actions.md verification), 0 blocked
- Source files in git diff: **10 files** (agent_kit/ledger.py, loop.py, ports.py, tool_kit.py, tools/communication.py, transport/discord.py, pyproject.toml, tests/store_contract_v1b.py, tests/test_no_leaked_secrets.py, ideas/sprint_1c_attachments.md)
- Tests: 85 tests passing per executor report

**Review quality:** Real review. Verdict: **needs_rework**. Found 2 genuine failures:
1. Leaked Supabase JWT still present in tracked plan-state files
2. False claim that JWT grep was clean
- Rework execution ran but was hampered -- reported "read-only" environment and couldn't make full changes. Only managed partial redaction.

**Idea coverage:** Core features implemented (Discord transport, Supabase store, coalescing, voice/image pipelines, status messages, mid-turn handling, reconciliation). The leaked secret issue was a security concern caught by review.

**Red flags:** 
- Leaked Supabase JWT in tracked files (review caught it, rework only partially fixed)
- Rework execution reported read-only sandbox, couldn't fully fix the issue

---

## Sprint 2a: Editorial core

**Goal:** Body parser/serializer, section editing, checklist, create/revert/render tools, history tools, expected_diff verification.

**Plan quality:** Good. 3 iterations. Critique caught: bootstrap path missing (how to create an epic when run_turn requires existing epic_id), _preamble/title addressing gap, parse vs validate confusion, prior_state contract for revert across time travel.

**Critique quality:** Strong. Found meaningful correctness issues about body parser strictness (spec says parse is lenient, plan made it strict), create_epic using raw args instead of parser output, read tools lacking operation_kind='read'.

**Gate quality:** Added value. Passed with 26 criteria generated from spec analysis.

**Execute quality:**
- Tasks: 21 planned, 21 done, 0 skipped, 0 blocked
- Source files in git diff: **19 files** (body.py, envelope.py, envelope.schema.json, loop.py, ports.py, editorial.py, editorial_reads.py, sqlite.py, supabase.py, templates.py, cli.py, __main__.py, migrations, tests)
- This was the best-executing sprint -- 8 batches, all tasks completed, substantial code produced.

**Review quality:** Real review. Verdict: approved. 0 issues, 0 rework items. All 21 task verdicts pass. Pre-check noted diff was larger than expected (2771 lines vs ~10 expected -- the heuristic was wrong, not the diff).

**Idea coverage:** Strong. Body parser with round-trip identity, section editing, create_epic with checklist seed, revert with transaction grouping, expected_diff verification, history tools, no-epic bootstrap path, all acceptance criteria addressed.

**Red flags:** None significant. Cleanest sprint of the five.

---

## Sprint 2b: Editorial polish

**Goal:** Feedback system, end-of-turn checks, search tools, agent observations, system prompt, show-changes pattern.

**Plan quality:** Adequate. Only 1 critique iteration (light robustness mode).

**Critique quality:** Found 2 real flags: (1) hot context would skip global style/process feedback on no-epic turns, (2) system prompt plumbing missing from ports/fake model/ledger. These were valid. But light robustness meant only one critique pass.

**Gate quality:** Minimal. Light robustness gate just said "ITERATE" with no detailed analysis.

**Execute quality:**
- Tasks: 14 planned (including 1 T13 user_actions.md check)
- Initial execution (batches 2-12): **12 done, 1 blocked, 0 skipped**
- Source files changed in initial execution: ~30 files (feedback.py, end_of_turn.py, prompts.py, system.md, body.py, loop.py, ports.py, multiple tests)
- Rework pass (batch 1 -- post-review): ALL 14 tasks skipped because review was auto-approved and sandbox was read-only
- **CRITICAL BUG:** The finalize.json only captured the rework pass and shows all 14 tasks as "skipped". The initial execution data was lost from the finalize record.

**Review quality:** **Auto-approved** (light robustness). Every criterion marked "Light robustness: auto-approved." No actual code verification occurred. No diff checked. No tests run.

**Idea coverage:** Based on the initial execution batches, substantial work was done: feedback table migrations, store adapters, search tools, end-of-turn checks, observation tools, system prompt, LLM eval scaffolding. But since review was auto-approved, there is zero verification that any of it is correct.

**Red flags:**
- Review was rubber-stamp auto-approved -- zero verification
- Finalize lost the initial execution results, only captured the empty rework pass
- Final.md shows all 14 tasks as unchecked (skipped), despite ~30 source files being written
- 1 task blocked (T11: LLM-graded eval blocked because it needs live API access)

---

## Sprint 3: Multi-epic + message search

**Goal:** Epic selection heuristic, switching, full-text search, reference resolution, user modes.

**Plan quality:** Adequate. 1 critique iteration (light robustness). Critique found 2 real flags: epic routing/locking timing issue, and search portability (must work on both SQLite and Supabase).

**Critique quality:** Decent for one pass. The routing/locking flag was genuinely important.

**Gate quality:** Minimal. Light robustness gate just said "ITERATE."

**Execute quality:**
- Tasks: 14 planned
- **1 done (T14 -- just auto-attributed .DS_Store files), 13 skipped, 12 blocked**
- Source files changed: **0** (only .DS_Store files)
- **COMPLETE FAILURE.** The executor could not run any shell commands (`pwd` returned exit code -1). Every task was blocked because the sandbox had broken shell access.

**Review quality:** **Auto-approved** (light robustness). Every criterion marked "Light robustness: auto-approved." This means the review approved a sprint that produced zero code.

**Idea coverage:** Zero. Nothing was delivered. No epic selection, no switching, no search, no reference resolution, no user modes. The sprint is marked "done" in state.json despite producing no code.

**Red flags:**
- **Sprint is marked "done" despite producing zero source code** -- this is the most critical pipeline failure
- Executor sandbox was completely broken (shell returned exit code -1)
- Auto-approved review passed a sprint with zero deliverables
- Finalize ran and produced a final.md showing 13/14 tasks skipped
- The pipeline did not detect or escalate the total failure

---

## Systematic Issues

1. **Sandbox reliability:** Sprint 1a had no git repo in sandbox (audit skipped). Sprint 1b rework had read-only sandbox. Sprint 3 had completely broken shell. This is the #1 problem -- unreliable execution environments.

2. **Auto-approve on light robustness is dangerous:** Sprints 2b and 3 used light robustness, which auto-approved review with zero verification. Sprint 3 was auto-approved with zero code produced. The pipeline needs a minimum bar even at light robustness -- at minimum, verify that the git diff is non-empty.

3. **Finalize loses initial execution data:** Sprint 2b's finalize only captured the post-review rework pass (all skipped), not the initial execution that produced ~30 files. The pipeline needs to aggregate across all execution passes, not just the last one.

4. **Sprint marked "done" despite total failure:** Sprint 3 is `current_state: "done"` with zero deliverables. There is no gate between execution and the "done" state that verifies the sprint actually produced output.

5. **Secret leak not fully remediated:** Sprint 1b's review caught a Supabase JWT leak in tracked files, but the rework pass couldn't fully fix it due to sandbox restrictions. The issue may still be present.

6. **Critique quality degrades with light robustness:** Sprints 1a and 1b (standard robustness) had thorough, multi-round critique that caught real issues. Sprints 2b and 3 (light robustness) had single-pass critique that found fewer issues.

**Summary scorecard:**

| Sprint | Tasks | Done | Skipped | Blocked | Source Files | Review | Verdict |
|--------|-------|------|---------|---------|-------------|--------|---------|
| 1a | 16 | 16 | 0 | 0 | 29 claimed (no diff) | Real | Good |
| 1b | 23 | 22 | 1 | 0 | 10 in diff | Real (needs_rework) | Good, leak issue |
| 2a | 21 | 21 | 0 | 0 | 19 in diff | Real | Best sprint |
| 2b | 14 | 12 | 1 | 1 | ~30 (lost from finalize) | Auto-approved | Code written, unverified |
| 3 | 14 | 0* | 13 | 12 | 0 | Auto-approved | Total failure |

*Sprint 3's single "done" task (T14) only auto-attributed .DS_Store files, producing no actual code.
